Board of Supervisor Minutes 09-25-2012 
Page 1 of 5 
 
NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS’ MINUTES 
TUESDAY, September 25, 2012 
 
PRESENT: David Tenney, Supervisor; Jesse Thompson, Supervisor; Jerry Brownlow, Supervisor; and J.R. 
DeSpain, Chairman were all present. Jonathan M. Nez was not present.  
9:00 a.m.  Call To Order: Pledge of Allegiance and Invocation was led by Supervisor Brownlow.  
CALL TO THE PUBLIC: There were no comments from the public.  
CONSENT AGENDA:  a) Minutes: September 7, 2012 Special Meeting; September 10, 2012 Emergency 
Meeting; and September 11, 2012  b) Approve Warrant  & Voucher Report  over $1,000 Report  c) Constable 
Reports: Winslow Precinct #2, August 2012; Snowflake Precinct #3, August, 2012; Show Low Precinct #5, 
August 2012; and Pinetop -Lakeside Precinct #6, August 2012  d) Request acknowledgment of receipt of the 
Proclamation issued by the Governor for the General E lection and call for publication of Proclamation pursuant 
to ARS 16-214 e) Name an existing road in the Pinedale/Claysprings area to “High Moon Road” in Section 19, 
Township 11 North, Range 20 East f) Letter regarding Resolutions on Regional Haze  g) Sympathy letter to the 
family of former Cochise County Supervisor Tommy Thompson and the family of Sheriff Larry Dever :  
Supervisor Brownlow made a motion  to authorize the Chairman to sign the items in the consent agenda as 
presented; motion seconded by Supervisor Tenney. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
EMPLOYEE RECOGNITION: Navajo County Recognizes Lois Hancock, Dawn Wilson, Dana Sipes, and 
Laurie Kimpell for their excellence in personal performance far exceed ing organizational expectations: LaRee 
Price recognized Lois Hancock, Dawn Wilson, Dana Sipes and Laurie Kimpell. LaRee shared that Dana Sipes 
was submitted by LuAnn Garbini. Dana has served Navajo County for 22 years  and she is the Chief Clerk and 
Judge Pro Tem in Show Low. LaRee spoke of the excellence in service provided by Dana. LaRee indicated that 
Laurie Kimpell was nominated by Doug Boyle and LaRee spoke of her excellence in service. LaRee indicated 
that Laurie has a superior level of quality control, high in tegrity, fresh and new  ideas. La urie works in the 
Probation Department. LaRee spoke of Dawn Wilson indicating that Dawn has a great ability to organize, create 
lists, works well with her co -workers and has a great deal of integrity. LaRee introduced Lois Hancock and 
stated that Lois was nominated by a combination of her co -workers in the Assessors Department . LaRee 
indicated that Lois t reats everyone wi th respect and is very accurate,  timely and gets the job done. She stated 
that Lois always goes above and b eyond. Judge Price spoke of Dana and her work ethic. He thanked Dana and 
expressed his appreciation for everything she does for him. Dale Nielson spoke of Dawn Wilson  and her work 
ethic. Arno Hall spoke of Laurie Kimpell and his appreciation for Laurie. James Jay ne spoke of Lois Hancock 
stating that she is very quiet but very effective. The Supervisors congratulated all of the recipients.  
Jonathan M. Nez, Supervisor is present at 9:18 a.m.  
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS:  
a. United Way Parking Spot for the month of October: Jason Moore drew the name of Sandra Peterson.  
b. Consideration and possible acceptance of the back tax land list and authorization to publish notice of the 
same scheduling the 2012 Back Tax Land On-Line Auction to begin on October 15, 2012 at 9:00 a.m. : 
Clerk of the Board, Melissa Buckley discuss ed the new idea of holding the Back Tax Land A uction, on-
line. She gave a brief presentation of the Public Surplus Auction website and requested that she be given 
permission to hold an on -line auction this year. She a lso requested that the Board approve the back tax 
land list and publication of the same. Supervisor Tenney made a motion to approve the back tax land

<!-- page 2 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 09-25-2012 
Page 2 of 5 
 
list and authorization to publish notice of the same scheduling the 2012 Back Tax Land On -Line 
Auction to begin on October 15, 2012 at 9:00 a.m.; motion seconded by Supervisor Thompson. Vote 
unanimous approving the motion.  
c. BOARD OF SUPERVISORS ACTING AS BOARD OF EQUALIZATION:  Consideration and 
possible approval of the Board of Equalization Hearing Officer’s recommendations on hearings 
conducted on September 18, 2012 for Petition for Review of Real Property Valuation : Supervisor 
Tenney made a motion to enter into the Board of Equalization; motion seconded by Supervisor Nez. 
Vote unanimous approving the motion. Melissa Buckley discussed the hearing officer’s 
recommendations on the 23 parcels. Supervisor Brownlow indicated that he had a conflict on parc el 
number 212 -30-029E, indicating that parcel is owned b y his brother -in-law. Supervisor Brownlow 
made a motion to approve the Board of Equalization Hearing Officer’s recommendations on hearings 
conducted on September 18, 2012 for Petition for Review of Real Property Valuations; motion seconded 
by Supervisor Nez. Vote unanimous approving the motion. Supervisor Tenney made a motion to 
return to regular session; motion seconded by Supervisor Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the 
motion.  
HUMAN RESOURCES: Consideration and possible approval of Personnel Actions: David Coolidge reviewed 
the 20 item personnel action report. Supervisor Thompson made a motion to approve the personnel action 
report as presented; motion seconded by Supervisor Nez. Supervisor Brownlow noted that item number 4; the 
employee salary would be  paid for by the five Eastern Organization C ounties. Mr. Jayne, County Manager, 
indicated that yes, that salary is paid for by a grant and the employee would report directly to the county 
manager. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC HEALTH SERVICES DISTRICT Board of Directors Session : Supervisor 
Brownlow made a motion to enter into the Navajo County Public Health Services District Board of Directors 
Session; motion seconded by Supervisor Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
a. CONSENT AGENDA:  
i) Memorandum of Agreement between Navajo County Public Health Services District and Cedar 
Unified School District effective October 1, 2012 to September 31, 2015 
ii) Memorandum of Agreemen t between Navajo County Public Health Services District and 
Heber/Overgaard Unified School District effective October 1, 2012 to September 31, 2015 
iii) Memorandum of Agreement between Navajo County Public Health Services District and Holbrook 
Unified School District  effective October 1, 2012 to September 31, 2015 
iv) Memorandum of Agreement between Navajo County Public Health Services District and 
Lukachukai Community School effective October 1, 2012 to September 31, 2015 
v) Memorandum of Agreement between Navajo Cou nty Public Health Services District and Navajo 
Evangelical Lutheran Mission School  effective October 1, 2012 to September 31, 2015 
vi) Memorandum of Agreement between Navajo County Public Health Services District and Seba 
Dalkai Boarding School effective October 1, 2012 to September 31, 2015 
vii) Memorandum of Agreement between Navajo County Public Health Services District and Sequoia 
Village School effective October 1, 2012 to September 31, 2015 
viii) Memorandum of Agreement between Navajo County Public Health Services District and Show 
Low Unified School District  effective October 1, 2012 to September 31, 2015 
ix) Memorandum of Agreement between Navajo County Public Health Services District and Snowflake 
Unified School District effective October 1, 2012 to September 31, 2015

Board of Supervisor Minutes 09-25-2012 
Page 3 of 5 
 
x) Memorandum of Agreement between Navajo County Public Health Services District and Successful 
Beginnings Charter School effective October 1, 2012 to September 31, 2015 
xi) Memorandum of Agreement between Navajo County Public Health Services District and Sun Valley 
Indian School effective October 1, 2012 to September 31, 2015 
xii) Memorandum of Agreement between Navajo County Public Health Services District and Window 
Rock Unified School District  effective October 1, 2012 to September 31, 2015 
xiii) Lease Agreement betw een Baldwin Jones Properties, LLC and Navajo County Public Health 
Services District for office space in Snowflake for the WIC and Nutrition Programs for October 1, 
2012 to September 30, 2013 
xiv) Amendment #4 of Contract #HG050273 with the Arizona Department of  Health Services for 
WIC Program Services effective 10/1/12 to 9/30/13 for $299,656.00 
Supervisor Brownlow made a motion to approve the consent agenda as presented; motion seconded by 
Supervisor Tenney. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
b. Consideration and possible award of a contract, to purchase six (6) new 2013 Ford Escapes, to SSVT 
Motors, Inc., DBA Peoria Ford in the amount of $24,499 each : Mary Springer discussed the item and 
asked that Peoria Ford be awarded the contract. Mrs. Springer indicated th at this is a lease purchase 
program. Supervisor Brownlow asked if the money for the vehicles is coming from the H ealth District 
or First Things First  Grant. Dr. Kartchner indicated that the money would come from the F irst Things 
First Grant. Supervisor Nez asked if there was someone that accepted the position in Kayenta and if 
they would be receiving one of these vehicles. Mary Tyler stated that they are in the process of 
recruiting several positions for our Navajo Nation area, 4 Health Educators and 1 program coordinator. 
She indicated that they currently have 1 health educator and yes the vehicles would be driven by those 
employees. Supervisor Tenney  made a motion to award a contract, to purchase six new 2013 Ford 
Escapes to SSVT Motors, Inc., DBA Peoria F ord in the amount of $24,499 each; motion seconded by 
Supervisor Nez. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
Supervisor Tenney made a motion to return to regular session; motion seconded by Supervisor 
Brownlow. Vote unanimous approving the motion.   
TREASURER: Consideration and possible approval of abatement of personal property taxes : Manny 
Hernandez discussed the item  and indicated that there are some manufactured homes that have been unable to 
be located. Supervisor Tenney made a motion to approve the abatement of personal property taxes; motion 
seconded by Supervisor Brownlow. Vote unanimous approving the motion.   
JUDICIAL: Consideration and possible adoption of Resolution 34-12, authorizing a Home Detention / 
Electronic Monitoring Program: Judge David Widmaier, Presiding Justice of the P eace, discussed the item . He 
indicated that the item would allow the defendant to stay at home and maintain a job as long as they are alcohol 
free. He further stated that the e lectronic device would remain on their ankle; they are n ot confined to their 
home so they can work. Judge Widmaier indicated that the Court can set the boundaries and further indicated 
that there is no funding based on state statue the defendant pays for the device. Judge Widmaier indicated that 
the defendant can choose the vendor and the county is not involved , if the defendant quits wearing the device or 
violated the terms then the Court could resentence the defendant to jail. Supervisor Thompson asked about 
defendants from the reservation. Judge Widmaier indicated that the defendants have to have a land line or come 
to the court to download the information; he indicated that most of the defendants from the reservation go to the 
Tribal C ourts. Supervisor Thompson made a motion  to adopt Resolution Number 34 -12, authorizing a 
Home Detention/Electronic Monitoring Program; motion seconded by Supervisor Brownlow. Vote unanimous 
approving the motion.

<!-- page 4 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 09-25-2012 
Page 4 of 5 
 
PUBLIC WORKS: 
a. Consideration and possible approval of expenditures not to exceed $25 ,000 from District I Special Road 
Funds to f und emergency road repairs for t he Navajo Nation : Homero Vela discussed the item. 
Supervisor Nez indicated that Navajo Nation did issu e a state of emergency for the Western A gency 
area due to the amount of flooding received. Supervisor Nez extended his appreciation to Public Works 
for the help that District I had received. Supervisor Nez made a motion to approve the expenditures not 
to exceed $25,000 from District I Special Road Funds to fund emergency road repair s for the Navajo 
Nation; motion seconded by Supervisor Thompson . Vote unanimous approving the motion. Homero 
Vela recognized Martinez Mike, Randall Whipple and Willie Wilbanks for their work on this project.  
b. Consideration and possible approval to reject C ontract #B12 -07-020 Purchase of (1) or more 
conventional 10 Wheel Dump Trucks and authorize the Public Works Department to re -bid this project: 
Homero Vela discussed the item and reviewed the d iscrepancies in the bid specifications. Supervisor 
Tenney made a motion to reject Contract #B12-07-020 purchase of one or more conventional 10 wheel 
dump trucks and authorized the Public Works Department to re -bid the project; motion seconded by 
Supervisor Nez. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
c. Consideration and possible award of Contract #B12 -08-007 Supply and Delivery of Roadway Materials 
to the Navajo Nation to Hatch Constructio n and Paving, Inc. as presented : Homero Vela discussed the 
item. Supervisor Nez made a motion to award Contract #B12 -08-007 supply and delivery of roadway 
materials to the Navajo Nation to Hatch Construction and Paving, Inc. ; motion seconded by Supervisor 
Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
d. Consideration and possible award of Contract #B12 -08-008 Supply of Aggregate for Cover Material to 
Brimhall Sand and Rock and Building Materials, Inc. and Hatch Construction & Paving, Inc., effective 
October 11, 2012 : Homero Vela discussed the item . Supervisor Tenney made a motion to award 
Contract #B12-08-008 supply of aggregate for cover m aterial to Brimhall Sand and Rock and Building 
Materials, Inc. and Hatch Construction & Paving, Inc. effective October 11, 2012; motion seconded by 
Supervisor Brownlow. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
e. Consideration and possible award of Contract #B12 -08-009 Excavate and Crush Red Rock Material at 
the Peabody Western Coal Company to Schuck Development Corporation : Homero Vela discussed the 
item and indicated that 10 chapters are partnering with us for the crushing at $10,000 each. Supervisor 
Nez made a  motion to award Contract #B12 -08-009 excavate and crush red rock material at the 
Peabody Western Coal Company to Schuck Development Corporation; motion seconded by Supervisor 
Brownlow. Supervisor Brownlow indicated that this is a good example of partnership when the chapters 
pitch in with Navajo County. Homero it is a great partnership. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE: Report from Board members, County Attorney and County Manager : 
Supervisor Nez reported on the rain and flooding in District I. He expressed his gratitude for the help  from the 
county and spoke of the Dilkon fair. Supervisor Thompson reported on th e 9-11 events that he attended and 
indicated that the Southwest Navajo Nation Fair was smaller than in previous years h owever the activities were 
very nice. He further spoke of the activities  and the N avajo County Fair opening ceremonies. Supervisor 
Tenney reported on the 4-FRI meetings, EECO meeting and Small C ounties Forum. He indicated that CSA 
interviewed candidates and chose Pascal Berlioux  for the Executive Director of EACO & EECO . Supervisor 
Brownlow reported from District V  and thanked Homero Vela and Public Works for their work on the Blue 
Ridge Public School bus stop. He indicated that this was a partnership with the Apache Tribe and has helped the 
students and the parents. Chairman DeSpain reported on the Navajo County Fair. He indicated that it was a 
great fair this year. He thanked all of those that worked overtime and further thanked the Honor Guard.

ADJOURN: At 10:34 a.m.  Supervisor Thompson  made a motion to adjourn; motion seconded by 
Supervisor Brownlow. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
 
 
APPROVED: 
 
 
J.R. DeSpain, Chairman 
 
 
ATTEST:  
 
 
DATE:  October 9, 2012 
 
Melissa W. Buckley - Clerk of the Board
